be a finite subset of
V\cup\{\mathfrak{g}\}
. Let
A,B\in\mathcal{M}(\Lambda)
, and consider a pair of currents
(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2})
on
\Lambda
satisfying
(\partial\mathbf{n}_{1},\partial\mathbf{n}_{2})=(\partial A,\partial B)
. We write
\mathbf{n}=\mathbf{n}_{1}+\mathbf{n}_{2}
. Recall that up to relabelling the elements of
\mathcal{B}_{z}^{A,B}(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2})
, the measure
\rho_{z,\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2}}^{A,B}
only depends on
(\Delta\mathbf{n}_{1}(z),\Delta\mathbf{n}_{2}(z),A_{z},B_{z})
. The following proposition is the main result of this section.

Proposition 4.1
.

For every integer
M\geq 1
, there exists a constant
\kappa=\kappa(M)>0
such that for every
A
,
B
,
\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2}
as above, and
z\in\Lambda
satisfying
\max(A_{z},B_{z},\Delta\mathbf{n}(z))\leq M
, we have

\rho_{z,\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2}}^{A,B}(P)\geq\kappa,

for all
P\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2}}^{A,B}(z)
. In particular,

\rho_{z,\mathbf{n}_{1}}^{A}(P)\geq\kappa,

for all
P\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathbf{n}_{1}}^{A}(z)
.

Remark 4.2
.

The occurrence of the partition consisting of one element, i.e. the partition for which all the points of the block are tangled together, plays the role of an insertion tolerance property inside the block
\mathcal{B}_{z}
of
\mathcal{H}^{A,B}(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})
. We call this event
ECT(z)
. Proposition
4.1
implies, in particular, that
\textup{ECT}(z)
occurs with positive probability.

Let us recall that
\rho_{z,\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2}}^{A,B}
is constructed as the weak subsequential limit of the probability measures
\rho^{\tilde{S},\tilde{T}}_{z,N}
where
S=\Delta\mathbf{n}_{1}(z)+A_{z}
and
T=\Delta\mathbf{n}_{2}(z)+B_{z}
(recall that
\tilde{S}\cap\tilde{T}=\emptyset
by our choice of projection, as explained in Definition
3.33
). This convergence is in fact strong, i.e. pointwise on events, since the underlying space is finite dimensional. The measures in the prelimit are themselves defined as the pushforward of the block-spin double current measures
\mathbb{P}_{K_{N}}^{\tilde{S},\tilde{T}}
under the partitions on sources induced by the clusters of
\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{1}+\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{2}\sim\mathbb{P}_{K_{N}}^{\tilde{S},\tilde{T}}
.

A fundamental step to proving Proposition
4.1
is understanding the large
N
asymptotics of the size of the cluster of a source. We then use this to prove two lemmas that are used in the proof of Proposition
4.1
. The first one asserts that pairings occur with positive probability, where a
pairing
of
\tilde{S}
is a partition of it into sets of cardinality
2
, and the second one can be seen as merging the clusters of the sources at finite cost. In the following and later on this in section, we make the dependency on
a
explicit as it is of great importance.

4.1
Large
N
asymptotics of the size of the cluster

Let
S\in\mathbb{N}
be finite and recall that, for each
N\geq S
,
\tilde{S}
is its natural injection into
K_{N}
. Given a vertex
x\in\tilde{S}
, we write
\mathcal{C}_{x}=\mathcal{C}_{x}(\tilde{\mathbf{n}})
for the connected component of
x
in
\tilde{\mathbf{n}}
.

Proposition 4.3
.

Let
S=2k
,
k>0
, and let
a_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
.
Then there exists a constant
C=C(k,a_{0})>0
such that for every
x\in\tilde{S}
and every
N\geq 2k
, we have

\sup_{a\geq a_{0}}\mathbb{E}^{\tilde{S},\emptyset}_{K_{N},a}\left[\frac{|\mathcal{C}_{x}|}{\sqrt{N}}\right]\leq C.

Proof.

Note that for every
N\geq 2k
, we trivially have

\sup_{a\in I}\mathbb{E}^{\tilde{S},\emptyset}_{K_{N},a}\left[\frac{|\mathcal{C}_{x}|}{\sqrt{N}}\right]\leq\sqrt{N},

thus it suffices to handle the case of large
N
.
Let
u\not\in\tilde{S}
. Then, for every
a\geq a_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\tilde{S},\emptyset}_{K_{N},a}\left[\frac{|\mathcal{C}_{x}|}{\sqrt{N}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\left((N-2k)\mathbb{P}^{\tilde{S},\emptyset}_{K_{N},a}[x\longleftrightarrow u]+O(1)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{N-2k}{\sqrt{N}}\frac{\langle\sigma_{\tilde{S}\triangle\{x,u\}}\rangle_{K_{N},a}\langle\sigma_{x}\sigma_{u}\rangle_{K_{N},a}}{\langle\sigma_{\tilde{S}}\rangle_{K_{N},a}}+O(1/\sqrt{N})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{N-2k}{\sqrt{N}}\langle\sigma_{x}\sigma_{u}\rangle_{K_{N},a_{0}}+O(1/\sqrt{N})

\displaystyle\underset{N\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\sqrt{12g}\langle\varphi^{2}\rangle_{0,a_{0}}.

In the first line, we used the symmetry of the model. In the second line, we used the random current representation and the usual switching lemma for the Ising model. In the third line, we used that
\langle\sigma_{\tilde{S}\Delta\{x,u\}}\rangle_{K_{N},a}=\langle\sigma_{\tilde{S}}\rangle_{K_{N},a}
by symmetry (recall that
x\in\tilde{S}
but
u\not\in\tilde{S}
), and the mononoticity of
a\mapsto\langle\sigma_{x}\sigma_{u}\rangle_{K_{N},a}
, which follows from differentating and applying Griffiths’ inequalities. Finally, in the last line we used Lemma
3.26
. The desired assertion follows readily. ∎

Remark 4.4
.

From the proof above, one can show that
\mathbb{E}^{\tilde{S},\emptyset}_{K_{n},a}\left[\frac{|\mathcal{C}_{x}|}{(c_{N}N)^{2}}\right]\rightarrow\langle\varphi^{2}\rangle_{0,a}
as
N\rightarrow\infty
. The scaling limit of the size of the rescaled cluster is obtained in
[
KPP24
]
.

We now make an observation
6
6

6

This observation is not new, see
[
Aiz82
, Lemma 11.2]
.
that is subtle and means that arguments involving exploration of clusters naturally change the underlying model.

Remark 4.5
.

Let
u\in\mathbb{R}
. The scaling limit of the Ising model on
K_{N-r_{N}(u)}
with interaction
d_{N}(a):=\frac{1}{N}\left(1-\frac{2a}{\sqrt{12gN}}\right)
, where
r_{N}(u)=\lceil u\sqrt{N}\rceil
, coincides with the
\varphi^{4}
single-site measure with parameter
a(u)=a+\sqrt{3g}u
in place of
a
. Indeed, writing
\tilde{N}:=N-r_{N}(u)
, we have

d_{N}(a)=\frac{1}{\tilde{N}}\left(1-\frac{2(a+\sqrt{3g}u)}{\sqrt{12g\tilde{N}}}+O\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{N}}\right)\right).

4.2
Pairings occur with positive probability

Let
\mathcal{P}
denote the (random) even partition of the source set
\tilde{S}
induced by the cluster of
\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{1}+\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{2}\sim\mathbb{P}_{K_{N}}^{\tilde{S},\emptyset}
. Recall that
a
is a parameter of the single site measure.

Lemma 4.6

(Pairings happen with positive probability)
.

Let
S=2k
,
k>0
, and let
I=[\ell,L]
be a compact interval. Then, there exists a constant
c=c(I,k)>0
such that for every pairing
P
of
\tilde{S}
and for every
N\geq 2k
,

\inf_{a\in I}\mathbb{P}^{\tilde{S},\emptyset}_{K_{N},a}[\mathcal{P}=P]\geq c.

Proof.

We prove the assertion by induction on
k
. The case
k=1
is trivial. Let us assume that the statement holds for some
k\geq 1
. We will prove it for
k+1
.

Note that for every
N\geq 2k+2
, there exists
\alpha_{N}>0
such that

\inf_{a\in I}\mathbb{P}^{\tilde{S},\emptyset}_{K_{N},a}[\mathcal{P}=P]\geq\alpha_{N},

hence we can assume that
N
is sufficiently large. To simplify notation, we identify
\tilde{S}
with
\{1,2,\ldots,2k+2\}
.
Consider a parameter
a\in I
, and let us write
P_{0}=\{\{1,2\},\ldots,\{2k+1,2k+2\}\}
.
Then, conditioning on
\mathcal{C}_{x}
, and letting
x\in\{1,2\}
,

\mathbb{P}^{\tilde{S},\emptyset}_{K_{N},a}[\mathcal{P}=P_{0}]=\frac{1}{\langle\sigma_{\tilde{S}}\rangle_{K_{N},a}}\sum_{\mathcal{S}\subset(K_{N}\setminus\tilde{S})\cup\{1,2\}}\frac{Z^{\tilde{S},\emptyset}_{K_{N},a}[\mathcal{C}_{x}=\mathcal{S},\mathcal{P}=P_{0}]}{Z^{\emptyset,\emptyset}_{K_{N},a}}.

Notice that for
P_{0}^{\prime}=\{\{3,4\},\ldots,\{2k+1,2k+2\}\}
,

\displaystyle Z^{\tilde{S},\emptyset}_{K_{N},a}[\mathcal{C}_{x}=\mathcal{S},\mathcal{P}=P_{0}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Z^{\{1,2\},\emptyset}_{\mathcal{S},a}[\mathcal{C}_{x}=\mathcal{S}]Z^{\tilde{S}\setminus\{1,2\},\emptyset}_{K_{N}\setminus\mathcal{S},a}[\mathcal{P}=P_{0}^{\prime}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Z^{\{1,2\},\emptyset}_{K_{N},a}[\mathcal{C}_{x}=\mathcal{S}]\frac{Z^{\tilde{S}\setminus\{1,2\},\emptyset}_{K_{N}\setminus\mathcal{S},a}[\mathcal{P}=P_{0}^{\prime}]}{Z^{\emptyset,\emptyset}_{K_{N}\setminus\mathcal{S},a}}.

Using the above equalities, we obtain

\mathbb{P}^{\tilde{S},\emptyset}_{K_{N},a}[\mathcal{P}=P_{0}]=\sum_{\mathcal{S}\subset(K_{N}\setminus\tilde{S})\cup\{1,2\}}Q_{\mathcal{S},N}(a)\mathbb{P}^{\{1,2\},\emptyset}_{K_{N},a}[\mathcal{C}_{x}=\mathcal{S}]\mathbb{P}^{\tilde{S}\setminus\{1,2\},\emptyset}_{K_{N}\setminus\mathcal{S},a}[\mathcal{P}=P^{\prime}_{0}],

(4.1)

where

Q_{\mathcal{S},N}(a):=\frac{\langle\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\rangle_{K_{N},a}\langle\sigma_{\tilde{S}\setminus\{1,2\}}\rangle_{K_{N}\setminus\mathcal{S},a}}{\langle\sigma_{\tilde{S}}\rangle_{K_{N},a}}.

Let us now consider a constant
C_{0}>0
such that

\mathbb{P}_{K_{N},a}^{\{1,2\},\emptyset}[A]\geq\frac{1}{2}\text{ for every }a\in I,

where
A=A(N)=\{|\mathcal{C}_{x}|<C_{0}\sqrt{N},\>(\mathcal{C}_{x}\setminus\{1,2\})\cap\tilde{S}=\emptyset\}
. The existence of such a constant follows from Proposition
4.3
and the symmetry of the model, provided that
N
is large enough.
Using Griffiths inequality yields that for every
\mathcal{S}
such that
|\mathcal{S}|<C_{0}\sqrt{N}
and every
a\in I
,

Q_{\mathcal{S},N}(a)\geq Q_{N}:=\frac{\langle\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\rangle_{K_{N},L}\langle\sigma_{\tilde{S}\setminus\{1,2\}}\rangle_{K_{N-\lceil C_{0}\sqrt{N}\rceil},L}}{\langle\sigma_{\tilde{S}}\rangle_{K_{N},\ell}}.

Since every summand in (
4.1
) is non-negative, using the above inequalities we obtain

\mathbb{P}^{\tilde{S},\emptyset}_{K_{N},a}[\mathcal{P}=P_{0}]\geq\frac{1}{2}Q_{N}\inf_{b\in I}\inf_{|\mathcal{S}|<C_{0}\sqrt{N}}\mathbb{P}^{\tilde{S}\setminus\{1,2\},\emptyset}_{K_{N}\setminus\mathcal{S},b}[\mathcal{P}=P^{\prime}_{0}]

for every
a\in I
.

Using Remark
4.5
and Lemma
3.26
we also obtain that

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}Q_{N}=\frac{\langle\varphi^{2}\rangle_{0,L}\langle\varphi^{2k}\rangle_{0,L+\sqrt{3g}C_{0}}}{\langle\varphi^{2k+2}\rangle_{0,\ell}}.

Finally, it follows from our induction hypothesis and Remark
4.5
that

\inf_{b\in I}\inf_{|\mathcal{S}|<C_{0}\sqrt{N}}\mathbb{P}^{\tilde{S}\setminus\{1,2\},\emptyset}_{K_{N}\setminus\mathcal{S},b}[\mathcal{P}=P^{\prime}_{0}]\geq c(k,I^{\prime}),

where
I^{\prime}=[\ell,L+\sqrt{3g}C_{0}]
. This concludes the proof.
∎

4.3
Cluster merging at finite cost

Given a partition
P=\{P_{1},P_{2},\ldots,P_{n}\}
of
\tilde{S}
, we write
P^{i,j}
for the partition of
\tilde{S}
with
P_{i}
and
P_{j}
merged together, i.e.
P^{i,j}=(P\setminus\{P_{i},P_{j}\})\cup\{P_{i}\cup P_{j}\}
.

Lemma 4.7

(Cluster merging at finite cost)
.

Let
S=2k
,
k>0
. For every
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, there exists
\delta=\delta(\varepsilon)>0
such that the following holds for every
N
large enough.
Assume that for a partition
P
of
\tilde{S}
we have

\mathbb{P}_{K_{N}}^{\tilde{S},\emptyset}[\mathcal{P}=P]\geq\varepsilon.

(4.2)

Then for every
i,j
we have

\mathbb{P}_{K_{N}}^{\tilde{S},\emptyset}[\mathcal{P}=P^{i,j}]\geq\delta\mathbb{P}_{K_{N}}^{\tilde{S},\emptyset}[\mathcal{P}=P].

(4.3)

Proof.

Consider a partition
P=\{P_{1},P_{2},\ldots,P_{n}\}
satisfying our assumption (
4.2
). Fix
i,j\in\{1,2,\ldots,n\}
distinct and let
r\in\{i,j\}
. We start by defining
\Pi(P_{r})
to be the (random) set of (vertex-)self-avoiding walks where each walk in

\Pi(P_{r})
traverses only edges with non-zero value in
\tilde{\mathbf{n}}:=\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{1}+\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{2}
, and starts and ends in
P_{r}
.
We write
\pi(P_{r})
to denote the set of vertices visited by the self-avoiding walks in
\Pi(P_{r})
. It is possible that some vertices of
\mathcal{C}_{P_{r}}:=\bigcup_{x\in P_{r}}\mathcal{C}_{x}
are not contained in
\pi(P_{r})
, which can happen when
\tilde{\mathbf{n}}
contains a loop starting and ending at some vertex in
\pi(P_{r})
.

We first show that with high probability
\pi(P_{r})
has size
\Omega(\sqrt{N})
. Indeed, let
m>0
and
x,y\in P_{r}
, with
x\neq y
. For a self-avoiding walk
\gamma
in
K_{N}
which starts at
x
, ends at
y
, and has length
\gamma
, we write
\{x\overset{\gamma}{\longleftrightarrow}y\}
for the event that
\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{1}(e)>0
for all edges
e
of
\gamma
. When
\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{1}\in\{x\overset{\gamma}{\longleftrightarrow}y\}
, the current

\tilde{\mathbf{m}}_{1}(e):=\begin{cases}\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{1}(e)-1,&\text{for }e\text{ edge of }\gamma,\\
\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{1}(e),&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

has sources
\tilde{S}\setminus\{x,y\}
. Thus, using the inequality
\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{1}(e)}\leq\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbf{m}}_{1}(e)}
and arguing as in the proof of Proposition
4.3
, we obtain (for the single current measure)

\mathbb{P}^{\tilde{S}}_{K_{N}}[x\overset{\gamma}{\longleftrightarrow}y]\leq d_{N}^{m}\frac{Z_{K_{N}}^{\tilde{S}\setminus\{x,y\}}}{Z^{\tilde{S}}_{K_{N}}}=d_{N}^{m}\frac{\langle\sigma_{\tilde{S}\setminus\{x,y\}}\rangle_{K_{N}}}{\langle\sigma_{\tilde{S}}\rangle_{K_{N}}}\leq C_{1}d_{N}^{m}\sqrt{N},

where
d_{N}
is defined in (
3.4
),
m
is the length of
\gamma
, and
C_{1}=C_{1}(k)>0
is a constant.

Note that there are at most
N^{m-1}
self-avoiding walks of length
m
connecting
x
and
y
in
K_{N}
. Moreover, when
|\pi(P_{r})|\leq m
, each
x\in P_{r}
is connected to some
y\in P_{r}
by a self-avoiding walk of length at most
m
. Since adding a second current cannot decrease
|\pi(P_{r})|
, for every
1>\eta>0

\mathbb{P}^{\tilde{S},\emptyset}_{K_{N}}\left[\{\mathcal{P}=P\}\cap\left(\{|\pi(P_{i})|\leq\eta\sqrt{N}\}\cup\{|\pi(P_{j})|\leq\eta\sqrt{N}\}\right)\right]\leq C_{2}d_{N}\eta N\leq C_{3}\eta,

where
C_{2}=C_{2}(k)>0,C_{3}=C_{3}(k)>0
are constants. Thus, because of our assumption (
4.2
) there exists a constant
c>0
such that

\mathbb{P}^{\tilde{S},\emptyset}_{K_{N}}[\{\mathcal{P}=P\}\cap\{|\pi(P_{i})|,|\pi(P_{j})|\geq c\sqrt{N}\}]\geq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{P}^{\tilde{S},\emptyset}_{K_{N}}[\mathcal{P}=P]

(4.4)

for every
N\geq 2k
.

Let
(\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{1},\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{2})\in\{\mathcal{P}=P\}\cap\{|\pi(P_{i})|,|\pi(P_{j})|\geq c\sqrt{N}\}
. For every
x\in\pi(P_{i})\setminus\tilde{S}
and
y\in\pi(P_{j})\setminus\tilde{S}
, define
(\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{1}^{x,y},\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{2}^{x,y})
as follows. First, we identify
x
and
y
in the natural way, labelling the point obtained from the gluing by
x
. Also, we preserve the labelling of all other vertices. The gluing procedure gives rise to a pair of currents
(\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{1}^{x,y},\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{2}^{x,y})
which has the property that
\mathcal{P}=P^{i,j}
. The caveat is that they are defined on
N-1
vertices. To fix this, we add a new vertex, labelled
y
, and extend
\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{1}^{x,y}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{2}^{x,y}
in the natural way such that
y
is of degree
0
in their sum.

We define
f
as the multi-valued map which associates to each
(\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{1},\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{2})
the set of all possible
(\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{1}^{x,y},\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{2}^{x,y})
. Note that, since
|\pi(P_{i})|,|\pi(P_{j})|\geq c\sqrt{N}
, we have

|f(\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{1},\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{2})|\geq(c\sqrt{N}-|\tilde{S}|)^{2}\geq c_{1}N,

for some constant
c_{1}>0
, provided that
N
is large enough. Moreover, since no new edges were added,
\mathbb{P}^{\tilde{S},\emptyset}_{K_{N}}[(\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{1},\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{2})]=\mathbb{P}^{\tilde{S},\emptyset}_{K_{N}}[(\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{1}^{x,y},\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{2}^{x,y})]
. Thus, for every
(\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{1},\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{2})\in\{\mathcal{P}=P\}\cap\{|\pi(P_{i})|,|\pi(P_{j})|\geq c\sqrt{N}\}
,

\mathbb{P}_{K_{N}}^{\tilde{S},\emptyset}[(\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{1},\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{2})]=\sum_{(\tilde{\mathbf{n}},\tilde{\mathbf{m}})\in f(\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{1},\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{2})}\frac{\mathbb{P}_{K_{N}}^{\tilde{S},\emptyset}[(\tilde{\mathbf{n}},\tilde{\mathbf{m}})]}{|f(\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{1},\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{2})|}\leq\sum_{(\tilde{\mathbf{n}},\tilde{\mathbf{m}})\in f(\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{1},\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{2})}\frac{\mathbb{P}_{K_{N}}^{\tilde{S},\emptyset}[(\tilde{\mathbf{n}},\tilde{\mathbf{m}})]}{c_{1}N}

which, by Lemma
4.4
, yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{P}_{K_{N}}^{\tilde{S},\emptyset}[\mathcal{P}=P]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{1},\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{2})\in\{\mathcal{P}=P\}\\
|\pi(P_{i})|,|\pi(P_{j})|\geq c\sqrt{N}\end{subarray}}\sum_{(\tilde{\mathbf{n}},\tilde{\mathbf{m}})\in f(\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{1},\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{2})}\frac{\mathbb{P}_{K_{N}}^{\tilde{S},\emptyset}[(\tilde{\mathbf{n}},\tilde{\mathbf{m}})]}{c_{1}N}

(4.5)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{(\tilde{\mathbf{n}},\tilde{\mathbf{m}})\in\{\mathcal{P}=P^{i,j}\}}\frac{|f^{-1}(\tilde{\mathbf{n}},\tilde{\mathbf{m}})|}{c_{1}N}\mathbb{P}^{\tilde{S},\emptyset}_{K_{N}}[(\tilde{\mathbf{n}},\tilde{\mathbf{m}})].

We now estimate
|f^{-1}(\tilde{\mathbf{n}},\tilde{\mathbf{m}})|
. Let
(\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{1},\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{2})\in f^{-1}(\tilde{\mathbf{n}},\tilde{\mathbf{m}})
. Then, there exists
x\in\pi(P_{i}),y\in\pi(P_{j})
such that
\tilde{\mathbf{n}}=\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{1}^{x,y}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{m}}=\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{2}^{x,y}
. Consider the number of self-avoiding walks between vertices of
P_{i}
in
(\tilde{\mathbf{n}},\tilde{\mathbf{m}})
and the number of self-avoiding walks between vertices of
P_{j}
in
(\tilde{\mathbf{n}},\tilde{\mathbf{m}})
. Notice that
both
numbers strictly decrease when we remove
x
from
\mathcal{C}_{P_{i}\cup P_{j}}
and that no other point has this property. Since we do not relabel
x
in the construction of
(\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{1}^{x,y},\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{2}^{x,y})
, the topological consideration above implies that this point is the same for any
(\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{3},\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{4})\in f^{-1}(\tilde{\mathbf{n}},\tilde{\mathbf{m}})
. See Figure
2
. Thus,
\mathcal{C}_{P_{i}}(\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{1},\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{2})=\mathcal{C}_{P_{i}}(\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{3},\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{4})
.

Since the labels of all other vertices are preserved after gluing except for
y
, this means that any
(\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{3},\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{4})\in f^{-1}(\tilde{\mathbf{n}},\tilde{\mathbf{m}})
can be mapped bijectively to
(\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{1},\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{2})
by swapping the label of
y
with a vertex of degree zero in
(\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{1},\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{2})
. Thus,
|f^{-1}(\tilde{\mathbf{n}},\tilde{\mathbf{m}})|\leq N
. Putting this into (
4.5
) gives the desired result.
∎

Figure 2:
An example illustrating the merging of
\mathcal{C}_{P_{i}}
and
\mathcal{C}_{P_{j}}
.

4.4
Proof of Proposition
4.1

We prove the estimate for the case of a double current; the case of a single current follows similarly. Let
P
be an admissible partition of
\tilde{S}\sqcup\tilde{T}
.
Note that,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{K_{N}}^{\tilde{S},\tilde{T}}[P]=\frac{\langle\sigma_{\tilde{S}\sqcup\tilde{T}}\rangle_{K_{N}}}{\langle\sigma_{\tilde{S}}\rangle_{K_{N}}\langle\sigma_{\tilde{T}}\rangle_{K_{N}}}\mathbb{P}_{K_{N}}^{\tilde{S}\sqcup\tilde{T},\emptyset}[P,\>\mathcal{F}_{\tilde{T}}]=(1+o(1))\frac{\langle\varphi^{S+T}\rangle_{0}}{\langle\varphi^{S}\rangle_{0}\langle\varphi^{T}\rangle_{0}}\mathbb{P}_{K_{N}}^{\tilde{S}\sqcup\tilde{T},\emptyset}[P]

since any configuration
\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{1}+\tilde{\mathbf{n}}_{2}\sim\mathbb{P}_{K_{N}}^{\tilde{S},\tilde{T}}
that realises
P
immediately realises
\mathcal{F}_{\tilde{T}}
because of the admissibility constraint on
P
. This computation gives Lemmas
4.6
and
4.7
for the measure
\mathbb{P}_{K_{N}}^{\tilde{S},\tilde{T}}
. As a consequence, there exists a constant
c>0
such that, if
N
is large enough and if
P
is an admissible pairing of
\tilde{S}\sqcup\tilde{T}
,

\mathbb{P}^{\tilde{S},\tilde{T}}_{K_{N}}[\mathcal{P}=P]\geq c
. Note that by successively merging the partition classes of a pairing, we can obtain any admissible partition. Using Lemma
4.7
, we obtain that
\mathbb{P}^{\tilde{S},\tilde{T}}_{K_{N}}[\mathcal{P}=P]\geq c^{\prime}
uniformly in
N
(sufficiently large) for a constant
c^{\prime}>0
. Letting
N
go to infinity, we obtain the desired result.
\hfill\square

5
Infinite volume measures

5.1
Infinite volume distributions on currents

Denote by
(\Omega_{V},\mathcal{A}_{V})
the space of currents on
V
equipped with the
\sigma
-algebra generated by cylinder events (i.e. events depending on finitely many edges). For
\Lambda\subset V
finite, denote by
(\Omega_{\Lambda},\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda})
the space of currents on
\Lambda
equipped with its Borel
\sigma
-algebra. There are natural injections
\Omega_{\Lambda}\hookrightarrow\Omega_{V}
and
\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{A}_{V}
. Thus, we canonically identify measures on
(\Omega_{\Lambda},\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda})
as measures on
(\Omega_{V},\mathcal{A}_{V})
. Similarly, denote by
(\Omega_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}}},\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}}})
the space of currents on
\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}}
equipped with its Borel
\sigma
-algebra. We can also canonically identify measures on
(\Omega_{\Lambda},\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda})
as measures on
(\Omega_{V},\mathcal{A}_{V})
. We consider
\beta>0,h=0
fixed in this section and drop from it from notation when convenient.

Definition 5.1
.

For
\Lambda\subset V
finite, let
\mathsf{P}_{\Lambda}^{\emptyset}
and
\mathsf{P}_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}}}^{\emptyset}
be the probability measures on
\Omega_{\Lambda}
and
\Omega_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}}}
, respectively, defined by

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}_{\Lambda}^{\emptyset}[\mathbf{n}]=\frac{w_{\beta}^{\emptyset}(\mathbf{n})}{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{m}\in\Omega_{\Lambda}\end{subarray}}w_{\beta}^{\emptyset}(\mathbf{m})},\qquad\mathsf{P}_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}}}^{\emptyset}[\mathbf{n}]=\frac{w_{\beta,h_{\Lambda}(\mathfrak{p})}^{\emptyset}(\mathbf{n})}{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{m}\in\Omega_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}}}\end{subarray}}w_{\beta,h_{\Lambda}(\mathfrak{p})}^{\emptyset}(\mathbf{m})},

where
h_{\Lambda}(\mathfrak{p})_{x}=\sum_{y\notin\Lambda}J_{x,y}\mathfrak{p}_{x}
for
x\in\Lambda
.
We denote the expectations with respect to
\mathsf{P}_{\Lambda}^{\emptyset}
and
\mathsf{P}_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}}}^{\emptyset}
by
\mathsf{E}^{\emptyset}_{\Lambda}
and
\mathsf{E}^{\emptyset}_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}}}
, respectively. Later, when clear from context, we will drop
h_{\Lambda}(\mathfrak{p})
from the notation.

Proposition 5.2
.

The probability measures
\mathsf{P}^{\emptyset}_{\Lambda}
(resp.
\mathsf{P}^{\emptyset}_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}}}
) converge weakly as
\Lambda\uparrow V
to a probability measure
\mathsf{P}^{0}
(resp.
\mathsf{P}^{+}
) on
\Omega_{V}
. The convergence is in fact strong for cylinder events, i.e. for any
A\in\mathcal{A}_{V}
depending on finitely many edges,

\lim_{\Lambda\uparrow V}\mathsf{P}^{\emptyset}_{\Lambda}[A]=\mathsf{P}^{0}[A]\quad\text{ and }\quad\lim_{\Lambda\uparrow V}\mathsf{P}^{\emptyset}_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}}}[A]=\mathsf{P}^{+}[A].

Furthermore,
\mathsf{P}^{0}
and
\mathsf{P}^{+}
are invariant and mixing (hence, ergodic) under automorphisms in
\Gamma
7
7

7

Recall we fix a vertex-transitive subgroup
\Gamma\subset\textup{Aut}(G)
.
.

In order to prove the above proposition, we need the following lemma.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
\Lambda\subset V
finite. Then for every
x\in\Lambda
we have

\mathbf{E}^{\emptyset,\emptyset}_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}},\Lambda}[\Delta\mathbf{n}_{1}(x)+\Delta\mathbf{n}_{2}(x)]=4g\Big(\langle\varphi_{x}^{4}\rangle_{\Lambda}^{0}+\langle\varphi_{x}^{4}\rangle_{\Lambda}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Big)+2a\Big(\langle\varphi_{x}^{2}\rangle_{\Lambda}^{0}+\langle\varphi_{x}^{2}\rangle_{\Lambda}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Big)-2.

Proof.

For
k\geq 0
, let
u_{2k}:=\langle\varphi^{2k}\rangle_{0}
.
An integration by parts yields that

(2k+1)u_{2k}=2a\cdot u_{2k+2}+4g\cdot u_{2k+4}.

Note that for
i\in\{1,2\}
,

\mathbf{E}^{\emptyset,\emptyset}_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}},\Lambda}[\Delta\mathbf{n}_{i}(x)]=\dfrac{\sum_{\partial\mathbf{n}_{i}=\emptyset}(\Delta\mathbf{n}_{i}(x)+1)w^{\emptyset}(\mathbf{n}_{i})}{\sum_{\partial\mathbf{n}_{i}=\emptyset}w^{\emptyset}(\mathbf{n}_{i})}-1,

and

\displaystyle(\Delta\mathbf{n}_{i}(x)+1)w^{\emptyset}(\mathbf{n}_{i})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle w(\mathbf{n}_{i})\prod_{y\neq x}u_{\Delta\mathbf{n}_{i}(y)}\cdot(\Delta\mathbf{n}_{i}(x)+1)u_{\Delta\mathbf{n}_{i}(x)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle w(\mathbf{n}_{i})\prod_{y\neq x}u_{\Delta\mathbf{n}_{i}(y)}(2a\cdot u_{\Delta\mathbf{n}_{i}(x)+2}+4g\cdot u_{\Delta\mathbf{n}_{i}(x)+4})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4g\cdot w^{\{x,x,x,x\}}(\mathbf{n}_{i})+2a\cdot w^{\{x,x\}}(\mathbf{n}_{i}).

The desired result follows.
∎

Without loss of generality, we only prove Proposition
5.2
for
\mathsf{P}^{0}
. We first construct the limit on special cylinder events (which generate the
\sigma
-algebra) where both the bond parities and degrees of the current are locally specified. Fix
F\subset\mathcal{P}_{2}(V)
finite. Let
V(F)
be the set of
x\in V
which belong to an edge in
F
.
For
d_{F}\in(2\mathbb{N})^{V(F)}
, we consider the following event

\mathcal{C}_{F,d_{F}}:=\left\{\mathbf{n}\in\Omega_{V}\middle|\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbf{n}_{e}\text{ is even for all }e\in F,\text{ and }\\
\Delta\mathbf{n}_{F}(x)=d_{F}(x)\text{ for all }x\in V(F)\end{array}\right\},

where
\mathbf{n}_{F}
is the restriction of
\mathbf{n}
to pairs in
F
.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
F\subset\mathcal{P}_{2}(V)
finite and
d_{F}\in V(F)^{2\mathbb{N}}
. The probabilities
\mathsf{P}_{\Lambda}^{\emptyset}[\mathcal{C}_{F,d_{F}}]
converge as
\Lambda\uparrow V
. We define
\mathsf{P}^{0}[\mathcal{C}_{F,d_{F}}]
to be this limit.

Proof.

Let
\Lambda\supset V(F)
. Define
\mathring{V}(F)\subset V(F)
be the set of
x\in V(F)
such that if
J_{x,y}>0
for
y\in V
, then
y\in V(F)
, i.e. vertices whose
J
-support are contained in
V(F)
, which might be empty if
J
has infinite range.

For
\mathbf{n}\in\Omega_{\Lambda}
, define

w(\mathbf{n}):=\prod_{\{x,y\}\subset\Lambda}\frac{(\beta J_{x,y})^{\mathbf{n}_{x,y}}}{(\mathbf{n}_{x,y})!}\text{ and }Z_{\Lambda}^{A}:=\sum_{\partial\mathbf{n}=\partial A}w^{A}(\mathbf{n}).

We write
\mathbf{n}_{\Lambda\setminus F}
to denote the restriction of
\mathbf{n}
to edges in the complement of
F
and denote
Z_{\Lambda\setminus F}^{A}
for the corresponding partition function.

Observe that we can write,

\displaystyle w^{\emptyset}(\mathbf{n})\mathbbm{1}\{\mathbf{n}\in\mathcal{C}_{F,d_{F}}\}

\displaystyle=w(\mathbf{n}_{F})\mathbbm{1}\{\mathbf{n}_{F}\in\mathcal{C}_{F,d_{F}}\}\prod_{x\in\mathring{V}(F)}\langle\varphi^{d_{F}(x)}\rangle_{0}\times w(\mathbf{n}_{\Lambda\setminus F})

\displaystyle\times\prod_{x\in\Lambda\setminus\mathring{V}(F)}\langle\varphi^{\Delta\mathbf{n}_{\Lambda\setminus F}(x)+d_{F}(x)\mathbbm{1}[x\in V(F)]}\rangle_{0}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}_{\Lambda}^{\emptyset}[\mathcal{C}_{F,d_{F}}]

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{\mathbf{n}\in\Omega_{\Lambda}}w^{\emptyset}(\mathbf{n})\mathbbm{1}[\mathbf{n}\in\mathcal{C}_{F,d_{F}}]\mathbbm{1}\{\partial\mathbf{n}=\emptyset\}}{Z^{\emptyset}_{\Lambda}}=\frac{Z^{d_{F}}_{\Lambda\setminus F}}{Z^{\emptyset}_{\Lambda}}f(F,d_{F}),

where

f(F,d_{F}):=\prod_{x\in\mathring{V}(F)}\langle\varphi^{d_{F}(x)}\rangle_{0}\sum_{\partial\mathbf{n}_{F}=\emptyset}w(\mathbf{n}_{F})\mathbbm{1}\{\mathbf{n}_{F}\in\mathcal{C}_{F,d_{F}}\}.

As a result, we get

\mathsf{P}_{\Lambda}^{\emptyset}[\mathcal{C}_{F,d_{F}}]=\Big\langle e^{-K_{F}}\prod_{x\in V(F)\setminus\mathring{V}(F)}\varphi_{x}^{d_{F}(x)}\Big\rangle_{\Lambda,\beta}^{0}\,f(F,d_{F}),

(5.1)

where

K_{F}=\beta\sum_{\{x,y\}\in F}J_{x,y}\varphi_{x}\varphi_{y}.

Note that by Young’s inequality, for any
(\varphi_{x})_{x\in V(F)}\in\mathbb{R}^{V(F)}
,

e^{-K_{F}}\prod_{x\in V(F)\setminus\mathring{V}(F)}\varphi_{x}^{d_{F}(x)}\leq e^{\beta\big(\sup_{\{x,y\}\in F}J_{x,y}\big)\sum_{x\in V(F)}\varphi_{x}^{2}}\prod_{x\in V(F)\setminus\mathring{V}(F)}\varphi_{x}^{d_{F}(x)}.

(5.2)

The expectation of the right-hand side of (
5.2
) is finite for
\langle\cdot\rangle_{\beta}^{0}
(i.e. the infinite volume measure). Hence, the the left-hand side of (
5.2
) is integrable with respect to
\langle\cdot\rangle_{\beta}^{0}
. Since the left-hand side is a local function, the convergence as
\Lambda\uparrow V
follows by the DLR equations. The observations above yield that
\underset{\Lambda\uparrow V}{\lim}\mathsf{P}_{\Lambda}^{\emptyset}(\mathcal{C}_{F,d_{F}})
exists and is equal to

\Big\langle e^{-K_{F}}\prod_{x\in V(F)\setminus\mathring{V}(F)}\varphi_{x}^{d_{F}(x)}\Big\rangle_{\beta}^{0}\>f(F,d_{F}).

(5.3)

∎

Proof of Proposition
5.2
.

We first prove the existence of
\mathsf{P}^{0}
. Let
A\in\mathcal{A}_{V}
be a cylinder event. Consider a sequence of finite sets of vertices
\Lambda
converging to
V
. We show that
(\mathsf{P}_{\Lambda}^{\emptyset}[A])_{\Lambda}
is Cauchy. The limit does not depend on the sequence since, by Lemma
5.4
, all limit points have the same values on events
\mathcal{C}_{F,d_{F}}
, for any
F\subset\mathcal{P}_{2}(V)
finite and
d_{F}\in V(F)^{2\mathbb{N}}
.

Let
M\in\mathbb{N}
, and consider a finite
F\subset\mathcal{P}_{2}(V)
such that
A\in\mathcal{A}_{V(F)}
. Note that the collection
\mathcal{C}_{F}:=\{\mathcal{C}_{F^{\prime},d_{F^{\prime}}}:F^{\prime}\subset F,d_{F^{\prime}}\in V(F^{\prime})^{2\mathbb{N}}\}
is stable under intersection. Thus, by inclusion–exclusion, there exists
N=N(M)>0
such that

\mathsf{P}^{\emptyset}_{\Lambda}\left[\mathcal{A}\cap\mathcal{E}\right]=\sum_{n=1}^{N}p_{n,\Lambda},

where
\mathcal{E}=\left\{\sup_{x\in V(F)}\Delta\mathbf{n}_{F}(x)\leq M\right\}
, and
p_{n,\Lambda}
are proportional to probabilities involving finite intersections and unions of events in
\mathcal{C}_{F}
. By Lemma
5.4
,
p_{n,\Lambda}\rightarrow p_{n}
as
\Lambda\uparrow V
.

On the other hand, note that

\displaystyle\sup_{\Lambda:V(F)\subset\Lambda\subset V}\mathsf{P}^{\emptyset}_{\Lambda}\left[A\cap\mathcal{E}^{c}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\Lambda:V(F)\subset\Lambda\subset V}\sum_{x\in V(F)}\mathsf{P}^{\emptyset}_{\Lambda}[\Delta\mathbf{n}_{E}(x)>M]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{M}\sum_{x\in V(F)}\sup_{\Lambda:V(F)\subset\Lambda\subset V}\mathsf{E}^{\emptyset}_{\Lambda}[\Delta\mathbf{n}(x)]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(F,\beta)}{M}\underset{M\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}0,

where the first inequality is by trivial inclusion and a union bound; the second inequality is by Markov’s inequality; and the third inequality is by the moment bounds of
\Delta\mathbf{n}
in Lemma
5.3
together with the convergence of
\langle\varphi_{x}^{2k}\rangle_{\Lambda}^{0}
as
\Lambda\uparrow V
.

Hence,
(\mathsf{P}_{\Lambda}^{\emptyset}[A])_{\Lambda}
is Cauchy. Define
\mathsf{P}^{0}[A]
to be this limit. Since cylinder events generate
\mathcal{A}_{V}
, this extends to a unique probability measure on
\Omega_{V}
.

Now we establish the invariance under
\Gamma
. By (
5.3
) and the fact that
\langle\cdot\rangle_{\beta}^{0}
is invariant under
\Gamma
, we have that for all
x\in V
,
F\subset\mathcal{P}_{2}(V)
finite, and
d_{F}\in V(F)^{2\mathbb{N}}
,

\mathsf{P}^{0}\left[\gamma_{x}\mathcal{C}_{F,d_{F}}\right]=\mathsf{P}^{0}\left[\mathcal{C}_{F,d_{F}}\right].

\Gamma
-invariance then follows by a truncation argument as above.

In order to establish mixing, notice that (again by a truncation argument) it suffices to show that for two finite sets
F,F^{\prime}\subset E
,

\lim_{d_{G}(o,x)\rightarrow\infty}\mathsf{P}^{0}\left[\mathcal{C}_{F,d_{F}}\cap\gamma_{x}\mathcal{C}_{F^{\prime},d_{F}^{\prime}}\right]=\mathsf{P}^{0}\left[\mathcal{C}_{F,d_{F}}\right]\mathsf{P}^{0}\left[\mathcal{C}_{F^{\prime},d_{F^{\prime}}}\right],

(5.4)

where
\gamma_{x}\in\Gamma
is such that
\gamma_{x}\cdot o=x
. Taking
x
such that
d_{G}(o,x)
is large enough, we notice that

\mathcal{C}_{F,d_{F}}\cap\gamma_{x}\mathcal{C}_{F^{\prime},d_{F^{\prime}}}=\mathcal{C}_{F\cup(\gamma_{x}F^{\prime}),d_{F}\cup d_{\gamma_{x}F^{\prime}}}.

Thus,

\lim_{d_{G}(o,x)\rightarrow\infty}f(F\cup(\gamma_{x}F^{\prime}),d_{F}\cup d_{\gamma_{x}F^{\prime}})=f(F,d_{F})f(F^{\prime},d_{F}^{\prime}).

(5.5)

Moreover, by Proposition
2.10
, the restriction of
\langle\cdot\rangle_{\beta}^{0}
to even functions is ergodic under
\Gamma
. Hence.

\displaystyle\lim_{d_{G}(o,x)\rightarrow\infty}\Big\langle\prod_{y\in V(F\cup(\gamma_{x}F^{\prime}))\setminus\mathring{V}(F\cup(\gamma_{x}F^{\prime}))}\varphi_{y}^{(\Delta_{F}\cup\Delta_{\gamma_{x}F^{\prime}})(x)}e^{-K_{F\cup(\gamma_{x}F^{\prime})}}\Big\rangle_{\beta}^{0}

(5.6)

\displaystyle=\Big\langle\prod_{y\in V(F)\setminus\mathring{V}(F)}\varphi_{y}^{\Delta_{F}(x)}e^{-K_{F}}\Big\rangle_{\beta}^{0}\Big\langle\prod_{y\in V(F^{\prime})\setminus\mathring{V}(F^{\prime})}\varphi_{y}^{\Delta_{F^{\prime}}(x)}e^{-K_{F^{\prime}}}\Big\rangle_{\beta}^{0}.

Combining (
5.5
) and (
5.6
) yields (
5.4
).
∎

Remark 5.5
.

Note that connections to the ghost vertex
\mathfrak{g}
disappear in the weak limit. Indeed, for any sourceless current
\mathbf{n}
with
\mathbf{n}_{o,\mathfrak{g}}>0
, consider the configuration
\mathbf{n}^{\prime}_{o,\mathfrak{g}}=\mathbf{n}_{o,\mathfrak{g}}-1
and
\mathbf{n}^{\prime}_{x,y}=\mathbf{n}_{x,y}
otherwise and notice that
w_{\beta,h_{\Lambda}(\mathfrak{g})}^{\emptyset}(\mathbf{n})\leq\beta h_{\Lambda}(\mathfrak{g})_{o}w_{\beta,h_{\Lambda}(\mathfrak{g})}^{\{o,\mathfrak{g}\}}(\mathbf{n}^{\prime})
. Summing over all such
\mathbf{n}
we obtain

\mathsf{P}^{\emptyset}_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}}}[\mathbf{n}_{o,\mathfrak{g}}>0]\leq\beta h_{\Lambda}(\mathfrak{g})_{o}\langle\varphi_{o}\rangle^{\mathfrak{p}}_{\Lambda},

which tends to
0
by the integrability assumption on
J
.

In addition, no “long” edges remain in the weak limit. More precisely, fix
\Delta\subset V
and take any
n
large enough such that
\Lambda:=B_{n}\supset\Delta
. We claim that

\sum_{y\in\Lambda\setminus\Delta}\mathsf{P}^{\emptyset}_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}}}[\mathbf{n}_{o,y}>0]\leq C(\Delta),

where
C(\Delta)
is uniform over
\Lambda
and such that
C(\Delta)\rightarrow 0
as
\Delta\uparrow V
. Indeed, proceeding as above, we have

\sum_{y\in\Lambda\setminus\Delta}\mathsf{P}^{\emptyset}_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}}}[\mathbf{n}_{o,y}>0]\leq\sum_{y\in\Lambda\setminus\Delta}\beta J_{o,y}\langle\varphi_{o}\varphi_{y}\rangle^{\mathfrak{p}}_{\Lambda}\leq\beta\sqrt{\langle\varphi_{o}^{2}\rangle_{\Lambda}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\sum_{y\in\Lambda\setminus\Delta}J_{o,y}\sqrt{\langle\varphi_{y}^{2}\rangle^{\mathfrak{p}}_{\Lambda}}

where we used the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality in the last inequality. By Corollary
D.12
and Remark
D.13
there exists
C>0
such that
\langle\varphi_{y}^{2}\rangle_{\Lambda}^{\mathfrak{p}}\leq\sqrt{C\max(\log(d_{G}(o,y)),1)}
uniformly in
\Lambda
.
Hence, there exists
c_{1}>0
such that, uniformly in
\Lambda
and
\Delta
,

\sum_{y\in\Lambda\setminus\Delta}\mathsf{P}^{\emptyset}_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}}}[\mathbf{n}_{o,y}>0]\leq c_{1}\sum_{y\in V\setminus\Delta}J_{o,y}\sqrt[4]{C\max(\log(d_{G}(o,y)),1)}=:C(\Delta)

After a summation by parts, the fact that
C(\Delta)\rightarrow 0
is ensured by the integrability assumption on
J
, c.f. the calculation in Lemma
D.7
.

The above considerations imply that certain global properties, such as the degree of a vertex in the random current remaining even, is preserved in the infinite volume limit. This can be seen as a strengthening of weak convergence of these measures to a type of local limit.

5.2
Infinite volume distributions on tangled currents

For
\Lambda\subset V
finite, equip
\Omega_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}},\Lambda}^{\mathcal{T}}
with its natural Borel
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}},\Lambda}^{\mathcal{T}}
. Equip
\Omega^{\mathcal{T}}_{V,V}
with the
\sigma
-algebra generated by local (tangled) events, denoted by
\mathcal{A}_{V,V}^{\mathcal{T}}
. We canonically identify measures on
\Omega_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}},\Lambda}^{\mathcal{T}}
with their push-forward under the natural injection
\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}},\Lambda}^{\mathcal{T}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{A}_{V,V}^{\mathcal{T}}
, by viewing the ghost as the contraction of
\Lambda^{c}
to a single vertex. Recall also that we view
\Omega^{\mathcal{T}}_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}}}
(resp.
\Omega^{\mathcal{T}}_{\Lambda}
) as a subset of
\Omega^{\mathcal{T}}_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}},\Lambda}
equipped with its natural Borel
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{T}}_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}}}\subset\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}},\Lambda}^{\mathcal{T}}
(resp.
\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{T}}_{\Lambda}\subset\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}},\Lambda}^{\mathcal{T}}
).

Definition 5.6
.

For
\Lambda\subset V
finite, let
\mathbf{P}_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}},\Lambda}^{\emptyset,\emptyset}
,
\mathbf{P}_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}}}^{\emptyset}
, and
\mathbf{P}_{\Lambda}^{\emptyset}
be the probability measures on
\Omega_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}},\Lambda}^{\mathcal{T}}
defined, for
A\in\mathcal{A}^{\mathcal{T}}_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}},\Lambda}
, by

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}},\Lambda}^{\emptyset,\emptyset}[A]

\displaystyle:=\frac{\sum_{\mathbf{n}_{1}\in\Omega_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}}},\mathbf{n}_{2}\in\Omega_{\Lambda}}\mathbbm{1}\{\partial\mathbf{n}_{1}=\emptyset,\partial\mathbf{n}_{2}=\emptyset\}w_{\beta,h_{\Lambda}(\mathfrak{p})}^{\emptyset}(\mathbf{n}_{1})w_{\beta}^{\emptyset}(\mathbf{n}_{2})\rho_{\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2}}^{\emptyset,\emptyset}[A]}{\sum_{\mathbf{n}_{1}\in\Omega_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}}},\mathbf{n}_{2}\in\Omega_{\Lambda}}\mathbbm{1}\{\partial\mathbf{n}_{1}=\emptyset,\partial\mathbf{n}_{2}=\emptyset\}w_{\beta,h_{\Lambda}(\mathfrak{p})}^{\emptyset}(\mathbf{n}_{1})w_{\beta}^{\emptyset}(\mathbf{n}_{2})},

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}}}^{\emptyset}[A]

\displaystyle:=\frac{\sum_{\mathbf{n}_{1}\in\Omega_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}}}}\mathbbm{1}\{\partial\mathbf{n}_{1}=\emptyset\}w_{\beta,h_{\Lambda}(\mathfrak{p})}^{\emptyset}(\mathbf{n}_{1})\rho_{\mathbf{n}_{1}}^{\emptyset}[A]}{\sum_{\mathbf{n}_{1}\in\Omega_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}}}}\mathbbm{1}\{\partial\mathbf{n}_{1}=\emptyset\}w_{\beta,h_{\Lambda}(\mathfrak{p})}^{\emptyset}(\mathbf{n}_{1})},

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}_{\Lambda}^{\emptyset}[A]

\displaystyle:=\frac{\sum_{\mathbf{n}_{1}\in\Omega_{\Lambda}}\mathbbm{1}\{\partial\mathbf{n}_{1}=\emptyset\}w_{\beta}^{\emptyset}(\mathbf{n}_{1})\rho_{\mathbf{n}_{1}}^{\emptyset}[A]}{\sum_{\mathbf{n}_{1}\in\Omega_{\Lambda}}\mathbbm{1}\{\partial\mathbf{n}_{1}=\emptyset\}w_{\beta}^{\emptyset}(\mathbf{n}_{1})}.

Proposition 5.7
.

The probability measures
\mathbf{P}^{\emptyset,\emptyset}_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}},\Lambda}
,
\mathbf{P}^{\emptyset}_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}}}
, and
\mathbf{P}^{\emptyset}_{\Lambda}
converge weakly as
\Lambda\uparrow V
, respectively, to probability measures
\mathbf{P}^{+,0}
,
\mathbf{P}^{+}
,
\mathbf{P}^{0}
on
\Omega_{V,V}^{\mathcal{T}}
. The convergence is in fact strong for cylinder events, i.e. for any
A\in\mathcal{A}_{V,V}^{\mathcal{T}}
depending on finitely many edges and tanglings,

\lim_{\Lambda\uparrow V}\mathbf{P}^{\emptyset,\emptyset}_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}},\Lambda}[A]=\mathbf{P}^{+,0}[A],\quad\lim_{\Lambda\uparrow V}\mathbf{P}^{\emptyset}_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}}}[A]=\mathbf{P}^{+}[A],\quad\lim_{\Lambda\uparrow V}\mathbf{P}^{\emptyset}_{\Lambda}[A]=\mathbf{P}^{0}[A].

Furthermore,
\mathbf{P}^{+,0}
,
\mathbf{P}^{+}
, and
\mathbf{P}^{0}
are invariant and mixing (hence, ergodic) under automorphisms in
\Gamma
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we prove the result for
\mathbf{P}^{+,0}
. First observe that
\mathbf{P}_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}},\Lambda}^{\emptyset,\emptyset}
is obtained via the following coupling: first, sample
(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2})
according to the measure
\mathsf{P}_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{g}}}^{\emptyset}\otimes\mathsf{P}_{\Lambda}^{\emptyset}
. Then, independently on each block
\mathcal{B}_{x}^{\emptyset,\emptyset}(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2})
for
x\in\Lambda
, sample a tangling
\mathpzc{t}_{x}
according to the measure
\rho^{\emptyset,\emptyset}_{x,\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2}}
constructed in Proposition
3.12
. Thus, to define
\mathbf{P}^{+,0}
, we extend the coupling to infinite volume in the obvious way by using Proposition
5.2
.

The
\Gamma
-invariance and mixing is a direct consequence of Proposition
5.2
and the construction via the coupling.
∎

5.3
Uniqueness of the infinite cluster

We prove that samples under the measures
\mathbf{P}^{+,0}
,
\mathbf{P}^{+}
, and
\mathbf{P}^{0}
, viewed as their associated multigraphs (as defined in Section
3.2
)
\mathcal{H}^{\emptyset,\emptyset}(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})
,
\mathcal{H}^{\emptyset}(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathpzc{t}_{1})
, and
\mathcal{H}^{\emptyset}(\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t}_{2})
respectively, almost surely have at most one infinite cluster. We start by proving an insertion tolerance property for local events that are in
\mathcal{A}_{V}
, i.e. do not depend on the tanglings. This, together with Proposition
4.1
, implies a (weak) insertion tolerance property for local events in
\mathcal{A}_{V,V}^{\mathcal{T}}
(resp.
\mathcal{A}_{V}^{\mathcal{T}}
).

Definition 5.8
.

For
\Lambda\subset V
finite, define
\Phi_{\Lambda}:\Omega_{V}\times\Omega_{V}\rightarrow\Omega_{V}\times\Omega_{V}
as the map
(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2})\mapsto(\mathbf{n}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{n}_{2}^{\prime})
, where, for
x,y\in V
,

(\mathbf{n}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{n}_{2}^{\prime})\{x,y\}=\begin{cases}(2,0),\qquad&\text{ if }(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2})\{x,y\}=(0,0),\,x,y\in\Lambda\text{ and }J_{x,y}>0,\\
(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2})\{x,y\},\qquad&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

In particular,
\mathbf{n}_{2}^{\prime}=\mathbf{n}_{2}
. We similarly define a map
\Omega_{V}\rightarrow\Omega_{V}
that we still denote by
\Phi_{\Lambda}
.

Proposition 5.9
.

For
\Lambda\subset V
finite, there exists
c=c(\Lambda,\beta,J)>0
such that, for all
A\in\mathcal{A}_{V}
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}^{+}\otimes\mathsf{P}^{0}[\Phi_{\Lambda}(A)]

\displaystyle\geq c\mathsf{P}^{+}\otimes\mathsf{P}^{0}[A],

(5.7)

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}^{+}[\Phi_{\Lambda}(A)]

\displaystyle\geq c\mathsf{P}^{+}[A],

(5.8)

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}^{0}[\Phi_{\Lambda}(A)]

\displaystyle\geq c\mathsf{P}^{0}[A].

(5.9)

Proof.

We only prove (
5.7
) as the proof of (
5.8
) and (
5.9
) follows by similar considerations.
It is sufficient to consider
A
depending only on finitely many edges. Recall that
B_{n}
is the ball of radius
n
centred on the fixed origin
o\in V
. Let
N
be such that
A\in\mathcal{A}_{B_{N}}
. We show the estimate (
5.7
) holds for the measures
\mathsf{P}_{B_{n}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\},}^{\emptyset}\otimes\mathsf{P}^{\emptyset}_{B_{n}}
with constant uniform in
n>N
. The result then follows by Proposition
5.2
. Note that for every
(\mathbf{n}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{n}_{2}^{\prime})
we have
|\Phi_{\Lambda}^{-1}(\mathbf{n}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{n}_{2}^{\prime})|\leq 2^{|\mathcal{P}_{2}(\Lambda)|}
, hence

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}_{B_{n}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\},}^{\emptyset}\otimes\mathsf{P}^{\emptyset}_{B_{n}}[\Phi_{\Lambda}(A)]\geq\dfrac{2^{-|\mathcal{P}_{2}(\Lambda)|}}{Z_{B_{n}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\},B_{n}}^{\emptyset,\emptyset}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{n}_{1}\in\Omega_{B_{n}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\}},\mathbf{n}_{2}\in\Omega_{B_{n}}\\
\partial\mathbf{n}_{1}=\emptyset,\partial\mathbf{n}_{2}=\emptyset\\
\mathbf{n}\in A\end{subarray}}w_{\beta}^{\emptyset}(\mathbf{n}_{1}^{\prime})w_{\beta}^{\emptyset}(\mathbf{n}_{2}^{\prime}),

(5.10)

where we recall
\Phi_{\Lambda}(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2})=(\mathbf{n}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{n}_{2}^{\prime})
, and
\mathbf{n}=\mathbf{n}_{1}+\mathbf{n}_{2}
. By definition,

w_{\beta}^{\emptyset}(\mathbf{n}_{1}^{\prime})w_{\beta}^{\emptyset}(\mathbf{n}_{2}^{\prime})=w_{\beta}^{\emptyset}(\mathbf{n}_{1})w_{\beta}^{\emptyset}(\mathbf{n}_{2})\prod_{x\in\Lambda}\frac{\langle\varphi^{\Delta\mathbf{n}_{1}^{\prime}(x)}\rangle_{0}}{\langle\varphi^{\Delta\mathbf{n}_{1}(x)}\rangle_{0}}\,\prod_{\{x,y\}\subset\Lambda}\frac{(J_{x,y})^{2}}{2},

(5.11)

where the final product is over all
x,y\in\Lambda
such that
J_{x,y}>0
and
(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2})\{x,y\}=(0,0)
.
By Proposition
B.5
, there exists
c_{1}>0
such that, for all
k\geq 0
,
\dfrac{\langle\varphi_{x}^{2k+2}\rangle_{0}}{\langle\varphi_{x}^{2k}\rangle_{0}}\geq c_{1}
. Since
\Delta\mathbf{n}_{1}\leq\Delta\mathbf{n}_{1}^{\prime}(x)\leq\Delta\mathbf{n}_{1}(x)+2|\Lambda|
, we conclude that

\prod_{x\in\Lambda}\frac{\langle\varphi^{\Delta\mathbf{n}_{1}^{\prime}(x)}\rangle_{0}}{\langle\varphi^{\Delta\mathbf{n}_{1}(x)}\rangle_{0}}\geq\prod_{x\in\Lambda}\min\left(1,c_{1}^{|\Lambda|}\right)=\min\left(1,c_{1}^{|\Lambda|^{2}}\right)>0.

(5.12)

Inserting (
5.11
) and (
5.12
) into (
5.10
), we get the existence of
c=c(\Lambda,\beta,J)
such that, for all
n>N
,

\mathsf{P}_{B_{n}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\},}^{\emptyset}\otimes\mathsf{P}^{\emptyset}_{B_{n}}[\Phi_{\Lambda}(A)]\geq c\mathsf{P}_{B_{n}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\}}^{\emptyset}\otimes\mathsf{P}^{\emptyset}_{B_{n}}[A].

Taking
n\to\infty
concludes the proof.
∎

Definition 5.10
.

For
\Lambda\subset V
finite, define
\Phi_{\Lambda}^{\mathcal{T}}:\Omega_{V,V}^{\mathcal{T}}\rightarrow\Omega_{V,V}^{\mathcal{T}}
as the map
(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})\mapsto(\mathbf{n}^{\prime}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2}^{\prime},\mathpzc{t}^{\prime})
, given by
(\mathbf{n}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{n}_{2}^{\prime})=\Phi_{\Lambda}(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2})
and

\mathpzc{t}_{x}^{\prime}=\begin{cases}\{\mathcal{B}_{x}^{\emptyset,\emptyset}(\mathbf{n}^{\prime}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2}^{\prime})\},\qquad&\text{ if }x\in\Lambda,\\
\mathpzc{t}_{x},\qquad&\text{ otherwise},\end{cases}

i.e. in
(\mathbf{n}^{\prime}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2}^{\prime},\mathpzc{t}^{\prime})
, the event
\textup{ECT}(x)
happens for all
x\in\Lambda
. We similarly define a map
\Omega^{\mathcal{T}}_{V}\rightarrow\Omega^{\mathcal{T}}_{V}
that we still denote by
\Phi^{\mathcal{T}}_{\Lambda}
.

Proposition 5.11
.

If
A\in\mathcal{A}_{V}
is such that
\mathbf{P}^{+,0}[A]>0
, then
\mathbf{P}^{+,0}[\Phi_{\Lambda}^{\mathcal{T}}(A)]>0
for every
\Lambda\subset V
finite. A similar result holds for
\mathbf{P}^{+}
and
\mathbf{P}^{0}
.

Proof.

Recall that, conditionally on
\mathbf{n}
, the tanglings
\mathpzc{t}=(\mathpzc{t}_{x})_{x\in V}
are independent and the distribution of
\mathpzc{t}_{x}
only depends on
(\Delta\mathbf{n}_{1}(x),\Delta\mathbf{n}_{2}(x))
. Therefore, the result readily follows from Propositions
4.1
and
5.9
.
∎

Let
\mathfrak{N}
denote the random variable that counts the number of infinite clusters in
\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})
(resp.
\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathpzc{t}_{1})
or
\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t}_{2})
). Define for
\ell\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
,

\mathcal{E}_{\ell}:=\{\mathfrak{N}=\ell\}.

Note that by ergodicity one has that for all
\ell
,
\mathbf{P}^{+,0}[\mathcal{E}_{\ell}]\in\{0,1\}
. The following result establishes that almost surely there exists at most one infinite cluster.

Proposition 5.12

(Uniqueness of infinite cluster)
.

For
\mathbf{P}\in\{\mathbf{P}^{+,0},\mathbf{P}^{+},\mathbf{P}^{0}\}
we have

\mathbf{P}[\mathcal{E}_{0}]+\mathbf{P}[\mathcal{E}_{1}]=1.

Proof.

We only prove the result for
\mathbf{P}^{+,0}
, as the proof for
\mathbf{P}^{+}
and
\mathbf{P}^{0}
is analogous. We start by constructing a convenient sequence of finite domains
(\Lambda_{N})_{N}
as follows. For each
x\in V
, consider a sequence of vertices
\pi_{o,x}=(u_{0}=o,u_{1},\cdots,u_{k}=x)
with
J_{u_{i},u_{i+1}}>0
for all
0\leq i\leq k-1
, which exists by the irreducibility assumption. We then consider the domains
\Lambda_{N}:=\bigcup_{x\in B_{N}}\pi_{x}
.

We first establish that
\mathbf{P}^{+,0}[\mathcal{E}_{\ell}]=0
for
\ell\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{0,1\}
. Let
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
with
\ell\geq 2
and assume by contradiction that
\mathbf{P}^{+,0}[\mathcal{E}_{\ell}]=1
. Let
\mathcal{I}_{N}=\mathcal{I}_{N}(\ell)\subset\mathcal{E}_{\ell}
be the event that the
\ell
infinite clusters intersect the blocks of the vertices in
B_{N}
. Observe that
\mathbf{P}^{+,0}[\mathcal{I}_{N}]\underset{N\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}1
, so in particular there exists
N
such that
\mathbf{P}^{+,0}[\mathcal{I}_{N}]>0
. By Proposition
5.11
, we have

\mathbf{P}[\Phi_{\Lambda_{N}}^{\mathcal{T}}(\mathcal{I}_{N})]>0.

However, one can easily see that
\Phi_{\Lambda_{N}}^{\mathcal{T}}(\mathcal{I}_{N})\subset\mathcal{E}_{1}
, and thus
\mathbf{P}^{+,0}[\mathcal{E}_{1}]>0
, which is a contradiction with
\mathbf{P}^{+,0}[\mathcal{E}_{\ell}]=1
, as desired.

Now we establish
\mathbf{P}^{+,0}[\mathcal{E}_{\infty}]=0
.
Assume for a contradiction that
\mathbf{P}^{+,0}[\mathcal{E}_{\infty}]=1
. Let
\mathcal{J}_{N}
be the event that at least three distinct infinite clusters intersect the blocks of the vertices in
B_{N}
. Note that

\mathbf{P}^{+,0}[\mathcal{J}_{N}]\underset{N\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}1
,
hence there is some
N
, fixed throughout, such that

\mathbf{P}^{+,0}[\mathcal{J}_{N}]>0.

Given
\Lambda\subset V
and a double tangled current
(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})
, we denote by
\mathcal{L}(\Lambda,\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})
the lift of
\Lambda
in
\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})
, i.e. the subgraph of
\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})
spanned by
\cup_{x\in\Lambda}\mathcal{B}_{x}(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2})
.
We introduce the following notion of coarse trifurcation. Let
\mathsf{CT}_{o}
denote the set of
(\mathbf{n},\mathpzc{t})
such that:

-

\mathbf{n}(x,y)>0
for all edges
xy\in\Lambda_{N}
.

-

ECT
(x)
happens for all vertices
x\in\Lambda_{N}
.

-

The cluster of the origin in
\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})
breaks into at least three distinct infinite clusters by removing
\mathcal{L}(\Lambda_{N},\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})
.

One can easily verify that
\Phi_{\Lambda_{N}}(\mathcal{J}_{N})\subset\mathsf{CT}_{o}
, hence by Proposition
5.11
we have

\mathbf{P}^{+,0}[\mathsf{CT}_{o}]>0.

(5.13)

Furthermore, note that by
\Gamma
-invariance, we have
\mathbf{P}^{+,0}[\mathsf{CT}_{0}]=\mathbf{P}^{+,0}[\mathsf{CT}_{x}]
for all
x\in V
, where
\mathsf{CT}_{x}=\gamma_{x}\mathsf{CT}_{0}
, and
\gamma_{x}\in\Gamma
is an automorphism such that
\gamma_{x}o=x
. A vertex
x\in V
is called a coarse trifurcation if
\mathsf{CT}_{x}
occurs.

For each
x\in V
denote
\Lambda_{N}(x)=\gamma_{x}\Lambda_{N}
. Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and define
S_{n}\subset B_{n}
to be a set of (sparse) vertices such that
\Lambda_{N}(x)\cap\Lambda_{N}(y)=\emptyset
for all distinct
x,y\in S_{n}
, and
S_{n}
is maximal with respect to this property. Then

|S_{n}|\geq\frac{|B_{n}|}{|B_{K}|},

where
K>N
is large enough so that
\Lambda_{N}\subset B_{K}
.
Let
T_{n}=T(S_{n})
denote the set of coarse trifurcations in
S_{n}
, and
L_{n}
denote the set of edges in
\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})
between
\mathcal{L}(B_{n},\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})
and
\mathcal{L}(V\setminus B_{n},\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})
. We now consider the graph obtained by identifying all vertices of
\mathcal{L}(\Lambda_{N}(t),\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})
into a single vertex for each
t\in T_{n}
. It is standard that one can construct a subgraph of this new graph that is a forest, where every leaf corresponds to an element of
L_{n}
, while every element of
T_{n}
corresponds to a vertex of degree at least three. See e.g.
[
LP16
, Theorem 7.6]
.
Therefore, we have

|T_{n}|\leq|L_{n}|.

(5.14)

On the one hand, by (
5.13
) and
\Gamma
-invariance,

\mathbf{E}^{+,0}[|T_{n}|]=|S_{n}|\mathbf{P}^{+,0}[\mathsf{CT}_{0}]\geq c(N)|B_{n}|,

(5.15)

for some constant
c(N)>0
.
On the other hand, for every
m>0
we have, by
\Gamma
-invariance,

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}^{+,0}[|L_{n}|]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in B_{n}\\
y\in V\setminus B_{n}\end{subarray}}\mathbf{E}^{+,0}[\mathbf{n}_{x,y}]\leq\sum_{x\in\partial^{m}_{\textup{in}}B_{n}}\mathbf{E}^{+,0}[\Delta\mathbf{n}(x)]+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in B_{n}\setminus\partial_{\textup{in}}^{m}B_{n}\end{subarray}}\mathbf{E}^{+,0}[\Delta^{m}\mathbf{n}(x)]

\displaystyle\leq|\partial^{m}_{\textup{in}}B_{n}|\mathbf{E}^{+,0}[\Delta\mathbf{n}(o)]+|B_{n}|\mathbf{E}^{+,0}[\Delta^{m}\mathbf{n}(o)],

where
\partial^{m}_{\textup{in}}B_{n}:=\{x\in B_{n}:d_{G}(x,V\setminus B_{n})\leq m\}
and
\Delta^{m}\mathbf{n}(x):=\sum_{y\in V:d_{G}(x,y)>m}\mathbf{n}_{x,y}
. Notice that
|\partial^{m}_{\textup{in}}B_{n}|\leq|B_{m}||\partial B_{n}|
.
Moreover, recall that
c_{0}:=\mathbf{E}^{+,0}[\Delta\mathbf{n}(o)]<\infty
by Lemma
5.3
. In particular,
c_{m}:=\mathbf{E}^{+,0}[\Delta^{m}\mathbf{n}(o)]\to 0
as
m\to\infty
. Altogether, we have

\mathbf{E}^{+,0}[|L_{n}|]\leq c_{0}|B_{m}||\partial B_{n}|+c_{m}|B_{n}|

(5.16)

Combining (
5.14
), (
5.15
) and (
5.16
), we obtain

c(N)\leq c_{0}|B_{m}|\frac{|\partial B_{n}|}{|B_{n}|}+c_{m}.

Letting
n\to\infty
and then
m\to\infty
, we find that
c(N)\leq 0
, thus obtaining the desired contradiction.
∎

Remark 5.13
.

As a by-product of the above proof we also obtain that the infinite cluster (if it exists) in
\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})
has at most two ends almost surely. Indeed, if the infinite cluster has at least three ends, one can proceed as above to prove that coarse trifurcations happen with positive probability and then obtain a contradiction.

6
\Gamma
-invariant Gibbs measures

In this section, we prove Theorem
1.2
. Fix
\beta>0
, which we may sometimes omit from the notation without risk of confusion. By Theorem
2.15
, we first need to prove that the condition
4
therein holds. A straightforward argument then yields that under (
1.10
),
|\mathcal{G}(\beta)|=1
to complete the proof. We therefore make the following assumption for the rest of this section, and in the end, we derive a contradiction.

Assumption 6.1
.

Assume that there exist
x,y\in V
such that
J_{x,y}>0
and

\langle\varphi_{x}\varphi_{y}\rangle^{+}_{\beta}\neq\langle\varphi_{x}\varphi_{y}\rangle^{0}_{\beta}.

(A)

Under this assumption, we show that the following event happens with positive probability.

Definition 6.2

(Bridges)
.

We say that a pair
x,y\in V
is a bridge for a double tangled current
(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})
(resp. a tangled current
(\mathbf{n},\mathpzc{t})
) if there exist two vertices
\overline{x}
and
\overline{y}
in the blocks of
x
and
y
in the multigraph
\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})
(resp.
\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{n},\mathpzc{t})
) such that there is exactly one edge
e
between
\overline{x}
and
\overline{y}
, and furthermore the cluster of
\overline{x},\overline{y}
splits into two disjoint infinite clusters after removing
e
. We denote the event that
\{x,y\}
is a bridge by
\mathcal{A}_{x,y}
.

In what follows, we write
\{A\longleftrightarrow B\text{ in }\Lambda\}
to denote the event that
A
is connected to
B
in
\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})
(resp.
\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}(\mathbf{n},\mathpzc{t})
). To simplify the notation, for
x,y\in V
, we write
x\longleftrightarrow y
instead of
\mathcal{B}_{x}\longleftrightarrow\mathcal{B}_{y}
. Moreover, we identify a moment
A\in\mathcal{M}(V)
with the corresponding multiset it defines, where each
x\in V
appears as many times as the value of
A_{x}
, e.g. we identify the moment
A
defined as
A_{x}=A_{y}=2
and
A_{z}=0
otherwise, with
xxyy
.

Proposition 6.3
.

Assume (
A
). Then,

\mathbf{P}^{+,0}_{\beta}[\mathcal{A}_{x,y}]>0\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathbf{P}^{+}_{\beta}[\mathcal{A}_{x,y}]>0.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{A}^{f}_{x,y}
denote the event that there exist two vertices
\overline{x}
and
\overline{y}
in the blocks of
x
and
y
in the multigraph
\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})
(resp.
\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{n},\mathpzc{t})
) such that there is exactly one edge
e
between
\overline{x}
and
\overline{y}
, and furthermore the cluster of
\overline{x},\overline{y}
splits into two disjoint clusters after removing
e
that are both connected to
\mathfrak{g}
.
By Proposition
5.7
and Remark
5.5
, it suffices to show that there exists
c>0
such that
\mathbf{P}_{B_{N}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\},B_{N}}^{\emptyset,\emptyset}[\mathcal{A}^{f}_{x,y}]>c
and
\mathbf{P}_{B_{N}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\}}^{\emptyset}[\mathcal{A}^{f}_{x,y}]>c
for every
N
large enough.

Let
c:=\langle\varphi_{x}\varphi_{y}\rangle^{+}_{\beta}-\langle\varphi_{x}\varphi_{y}\rangle^{0}_{\beta}>0
and take
N
large enough so that
\langle\varphi_{x}\varphi_{y}\rangle^{\mathfrak{p}}_{B_{N}}-\langle\varphi_{x}\varphi_{y}\rangle^{0}_{B_{N}}
is larger than
c/2
. By the switching principle of Theorem
3.12
, we have

c/2<\langle\varphi_{x}\varphi_{y}\rangle^{\mathfrak{p}}_{B_{N}}-\langle\varphi_{x}\varphi_{y}\rangle^{0}_{B_{N}}=\langle\varphi_{x}\varphi_{y}\rangle^{\mathfrak{p}}_{B_{N}}\mathbf{P}^{xy,\emptyset}_{B_{N}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\},B_{N}}[\tilde{x}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.75pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.75pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.625pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.875pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}\tilde{y}\text{ in }B_{N}],

(6.1)

where
\tilde{x}=xa(1),\tilde{y}=ya(1)
are the extra vertices added in
\mathcal{B}_{x},\mathcal{B}_{y}
due to the sources. Notice that due to the sources constraint,
\tilde{x}
is connected to
\tilde{y}
in
B_{N}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\}
, in particular on the event in (
6.1
) both
\tilde{x}
and
\tilde{y}
are connected to
\mathfrak{g}
, and therefore
\{x,y\}
would become a bridge by adding an edge between
\tilde{x}
and
\tilde{y}
in
\mathcal{H}^{xy,\emptyset}(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})
. We now implement this modification rigorously.
First, take
M
large enough (depending on
c
only) such that

\langle\varphi_{x}\varphi_{y}\rangle^{\mathfrak{p}}_{B_{N}}\mathbf{P}^{xy,\emptyset}_{B_{N}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\},B_{N}}[\tilde{x}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.75pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.75pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.625pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.875pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}\tilde{y}\text{ in }B_{N},~\Delta\mathbf{n}(x)\leq M,~\Delta\mathbf{n}(y)\leq M]>c/4.

(6.2)

To each
(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})
in the event above, we consider the modified configuration
(\mathbf{n}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t}^{\prime})
constructed as follows.
First, set
\mathbf{n}_{1}^{\prime}(x,y)=\mathbf{n}_{1}(x,y)+1
and
\mathbf{n}_{1}^{\prime}(e)=\mathbf{n}_{1}(e)~\forall e\neq\{x,y\}
(notice that
\partial\mathbf{n}_{1}^{\prime}=\emptyset
). In order to define
\mathpzc{t}^{\prime}
, first set
\mathpzc{t}^{\prime}_{z}=\mathpzc{t}_{z}~\forall z\notin\{x,y\}
, which makes sense as
\mathcal{B}_{z}^{xy,\emptyset}(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2})=\mathcal{B}_{z}^{\emptyset,\emptyset}(\mathbf{n}^{\prime}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2})~\forall z\notin\{x,y\}
.
By seeing the previously extra vertices
\tilde{x},\tilde{y}
now as the endpoints of the added edge between
x
and
y
in
\mathbf{n}_{1}^{\prime}
, we construct a natural bijection
\psi_{z}:\mathcal{B}_{z}^{xy,\emptyset}(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2})\to\mathcal{B}_{z}^{\emptyset,\emptyset}(\mathbf{n}^{\prime}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2})
for
z\in\{x,y\}
.
We can then set
\mathpzc{t}^{\prime}_{x}=\psi_{x}(\mathpzc{t}_{x})
and
\mathpzc{t}^{\prime}_{y}=\psi_{y}(\mathpzc{t}_{y})
. One can see that
\{x,y\}
is a bridge in the configuration
(\mathbf{n}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t}^{\prime})
, with
\overline{x}=\tilde{x}
and
\overline{y}=\tilde{y}
— see Figure
3
. On the one hand, notice that the map
(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})\mapsto(\mathbf{n}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t}^{\prime})
is at most
(M+1)^{2}
-to-
1
due to the degree constraints. On the other hand, by (
3.2
) and Proposition
4.1
, the ratio between the probabilities of
(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})
and
(\mathbf{n}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t}^{\prime})
under
\mathbf{P}^{xy,\emptyset}_{B_{N}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\},B_{N}}
and
\mathbf{P}^{\emptyset,\emptyset}_{B_{N}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\},B_{N}}
, respectively, is bounded above by a constant
C=C(M)\in(0,\infty)
. All in all, we conclude that for
N
large enough,

\mathbf{P}^{\emptyset,\emptyset}_{B_{N}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\},B_{N}}[\mathcal{A}_{x,y}^{f}]>\frac{c}{4C\langle\varphi_{x}\varphi_{y}\rangle^{\mathfrak{p}}_{B_{N}}},

as we wanted to prove. For the single tangled current, simply notice that by the stochastic domination (
3.7
) we have
\mathbf{P}^{xy}_{B_{N}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\}}[\tilde{x}{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.75pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.75pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.625pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.875pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}}\tilde{y}\text{ in }B_{N}]\geq\mathbf{P}^{xy,\emptyset}_{B_{N}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\},B_{N}}[\tilde{x}{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.75pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.75pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.625pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.875pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}}\tilde{y}\text{ in }B_{N}]
, and the above proof adapts readily.
∎

Figure 3:
An illustration of the mapping used in the proof of Proposition
6.3
. On the left, an example of
(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})
satisfying the event
\{\tilde{x}{\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.75pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.75pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.625pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.875pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}}\tilde{y}\text{ in }B_{N}\}
; on the right, its image
(\mathbf{n}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t}^{\prime})
by the map. The red vertices represent
\overline{x},\overline{y}
.

Definition 6.4

(Flows)
.

Given two vertices
\tilde{x},\tilde{y}
in a multigraph
\mathcal{H}
, we define the flow
f(\tilde{x},\tilde{y},\mathcal{H})
between
\tilde{x}
and
\tilde{y}
in
\mathcal{H}
as the maximal number of edge disjoint paths between
\tilde{x}
and
\tilde{y}
in
\mathcal{H}
. We also define
f(\tilde{x},\infty,\mathcal{H})
as the maximal number of edge disjoint infinite path in
\mathcal{H}
starting from
\tilde{x}
.

For a double tangled current
(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})
(resp. a tangled current
(\mathbf{n},\mathpzc{t})
) on
V
and
x,y\in V
, we denote by
f(x,y,\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})
(resp.
f(x,y,\mathbf{n},\mathpzc{t})
) the maximum of
f(\tilde{x},\tilde{y},\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t}))
(resp.
f(\tilde{x},\tilde{y},\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{n},\mathpzc{t})
) over all
\tilde{x}\in\mathcal{B}_{x}
and
\tilde{y}\in\mathcal{B}_{y}
, and by
f(x,\infty,\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})
(resp.
f(x,\infty,\mathbf{n},\mathpzc{t})
) the maximum of
f(\tilde{x},\infty,\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t}))
(resp.
f(\tilde{x},\infty,\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{n},\mathpzc{t})
) over all
\tilde{x}\in\mathcal{B}_{x}
.

Lemma 6.5
.

Assume (
A
). One has

\displaystyle\lim_{d_{G}(o,y)\rightarrow\infty}\mathbf{P}^{+,0}_{\beta}[f(x,y,\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})\geq 2]

\displaystyle=0,

(6.3)

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}^{+,0}_{\beta}[f(x,\infty,\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})>2]

\displaystyle=0.

(6.4)

Moreover, a similar statement holds for the measure
\mathbf{P}^{+}_{\beta}
.

Proof.

Note that by ergodicity and Proposition
6.3
, almost surely there are infinitely many bridges. Also, there is a unique infinite cluster, which has at most two ends almost surely by Proposition
5.12
and Remark
5.13
. For any
(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})
satisfying these properties, it is proved in
[
Rao20
, Lemma 3]
that
\lim_{d_{G}(o,y)\rightarrow\infty}\mathbbm{1}\{f(x,y,\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})\geq 2\}=0
and
f(x,\infty,\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})\leq 2
. Then, (
6.3
) follows by dominated convergence and (
6.4
) follows directly. The proof for
\mathbf{P}^{+}_{\beta}
follows the same argument.
∎

Lemma 6.6
.

Let
x\in V
and
\beta>0
. If
\mathbf{P}^{+,0}_{\beta}[x\longleftrightarrow\infty]>0
, then
\inf_{y\in V}\langle\varphi_{x}\varphi_{y}\rangle^{0}_{\beta}>0
.

Proof.

Since
\beta
is fixed, we shall omit it from the notation. By the uniqueness of the infinite cluster, for every
y\in V
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}^{+,0}[x\longleftrightarrow y]=\mathbf{P}^{+,0}[x\longleftrightarrow\infty,\>y\longleftrightarrow\infty]+\mathbf{P}^{+,0}[x\longleftrightarrow y,\>x\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.75pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.75pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.625pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.875pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}\infty].

(6.5)

The first term converges to
(\mathbf{P}^{+,0}[x\longleftrightarrow\infty])^{2}
by the mixing of Proposition
5.7
, while the second term converges to
0
. Thus

\lim_{d_{G}(o,y)\to\infty}\mathbf{P}^{+,0}[x\longleftrightarrow y]=\big(\mathbf{P}^{+,0}[x\longleftrightarrow\infty]\big)^{2},

hence

\mathbf{P}^{+,0}[x\longleftrightarrow y]
remains bounded away from
0
uniformly in
x,y
. We wish to show that there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
x,y
such that

\mathbf{P}^{+,0}[x\longleftrightarrow y]\leq C\langle\varphi_{x}\varphi_{y}\rangle^{0}.

(6.6)

To this end, we shall work at finite volume.
For every
m>0
large enough so that
x,y\in B_{m}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}^{\emptyset,\emptyset}_{B_{N}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\},B_{N}}[x\longleftrightarrow y\text{ in }B_{m}]\leq\mathbf{P}^{\emptyset,\emptyset}_{B_{N}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\},B_{N}}[x\longleftrightarrow y]\leq\mathbf{P}^{\emptyset,\emptyset}_{B_{N}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\},B_{N}}[x\longleftrightarrow y\text{ in }B_{m}]

\displaystyle+\mathbf{P}^{\emptyset,\emptyset}_{B_{N}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\},B_{N}}[x,y\longleftrightarrow B_{m}^{c},x\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.75pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.75pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.625pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.875pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}y\text{ in }B_{m}],

hence sending
N
and
m
to infinity, and using the uniqueness of the infinite cluster, we deduce that

\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbf{P}^{\emptyset,\emptyset}_{B_{N}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\},B_{N}}[x\longleftrightarrow y]=\mathbf{P}^{+,0}[x\longleftrightarrow y],

which implies that
\mathbf{P}^{\emptyset,\emptyset}_{B_{N}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\},B_{N}}[x\longleftrightarrow y]
remains bounded away from
0
for every large enough
N
.
We can now find constants
M,C_{1},C_{2}>0
independent of
x,y
and
N_{0}=N_{0}(x,y)>0
such that for every
N\geq N_{0}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}^{\emptyset,\emptyset}_{B_{N}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\},B_{N}}[x\longleftrightarrow y]

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\mathbf{P}^{\emptyset,\emptyset}_{B_{N}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\},B_{N}}[\mathcal{E}]\leq C_{2}\langle\varphi_{x}^{2}\varphi_{y}^{2}\rangle^{0}_{B_{N}}\,\mathbf{P}^{A,\emptyset}_{B_{N}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\},B_{N}}[\mathcal{E}]

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\langle\varphi_{x}\varphi_{y}\rangle^{\mathfrak{p}}_{B_{N}}\,\langle\varphi_{x}\varphi_{y}\rangle^{0}_{B_{N}},

where
\mathcal{E}:=\{x\longleftrightarrow y,\>\textup{ECT}(x),\>\textup{ECT}(y),\Delta\mathbf{n}(x)\leq M,\>\Delta\mathbf{n}(y)\leq M\big\}
and
A=xxyy
. Sending
N
to infinity and using that

\langle\varphi_{x}\varphi_{y}\rangle^{+}\leq\langle\varphi_{x}^{2}\rangle^{+}<\infty

by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and
\Gamma
-invariance, we obtain that (
6.6
) holds.
It follows that
\inf_{y\in V}\langle\varphi_{x}\varphi_{y}\rangle^{0}>0
, as desired.
∎

6.1
Proof of Theorem
1.2

Proof of (
1.9
) in Theorem
1.2
.

Assuming (
A
), we have
\mathbf{P}^{+,0}[\mathcal{A}_{x,y}]>0
by Proposition
6.3
. In particular
\mathbf{P}^{+,0}[x\longleftrightarrow\infty]>0
, which implies

c_{1}:=\inf_{y\in V}\langle\varphi_{x}\varphi_{y}\rangle^{0}_{\beta}>0

(6.7)

by Lemma
6.6
.
We now consider two cases.

First case.

\mathbf{P}^{0}[x\longleftrightarrow\infty]>0
.
Equivalently, we have
\mathbf{P}^{0}[f(x,\infty,\mathbf{n},\mathpzc{t})\geq 1]>0
. We also know by Proposition
6.3
that
\mathbf{P}^{+}[\mathcal{A}_{x,y}]>0
, so in particular
\mathbf{P}^{+}[f(x,\infty,\mathbf{n},\mathpzc{t})\geq 2]>0
. Let
(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathpzc{t}_{1})\sim\mathbf{P}^{+}
and
(\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t}_{2})\sim\mathbf{P}^{0}
be independent and
(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})\sim\mathbf{P}^{+,0}
, then by (
3.7
) we can couple
(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t},\mathpzc{t}_{1},\mathpzc{t}_{2})
in such a way that
\mathpzc{t}\succ\mathpzc{t}_{1}\sqcup\mathpzc{t}_{2}
.
Using this stochastic domination and inserting the event
\textup{ECT}(x)
, we obtain

\mathbf{P}^{+,0}\big[f(x,\infty,\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathpzc{t}_{1})\geq 2,\>f(x,\infty,\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t}_{2})\geq 1,\>\textup{ECT}(x)\big]>0.

Since
\{f(x,\infty,\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathpzc{t}_{1})\geq 2,f(x,\infty,\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t}_{2})\geq 1,\textup{ECT}(x)]\}\subset\{f(x,\infty,\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})\geq 3\}
, we obtain a contradiction with Lemma
6.5
— see (
6.4
).

Second case.

\mathbf{P}^{0}[x\longleftrightarrow\infty]=0
.
We prove the following lemmas.

Lemma 6.7
.

Assume
\mathbf{P}^{0}[x\longleftrightarrow\infty]=0
. Then, for every
x,y\in V
there exists an integer
m=m(x,y)
such that

\mathbf{P}_{B_{n}}^{xy}[x\longleftrightarrow y\text{ in }B_{m}]>1/2

(6.8)

for all
n
sufficiently large.

Lemma 6.8
.

Assume (
A
). Then, there exists
c_{2}>0
such that, for all
x\in V
there exists infinitely many
y\in V
such that, there exists an integer
m=m(x,y)
such that

\langle\varphi_{x}\varphi_{y}\rangle^{\mathfrak{p}}_{B_{n}}\mathbf{P}_{B_{n}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\}}^{xy}[x\longleftrightarrow y\text{ in }B_{m}]>c_{2}

(6.9)

for all
n
sufficiently large.

Before proving Lemmas
6.7
and
6.8
, we conclude the proof of the main result.
Let
x,y,m,n
be such that both (
6.8
) and (
6.9
) hold. By taking the product between (
6.8
), (
6.9
) and
\langle\varphi_{x}\varphi_{y}\rangle^{0}_{B_{n}}
(which we can assume to be larger than
c_{1}/2
by (
6.7
)), using (
3.7
), and bounding
\Delta\mathbf{n}(x)
and
\Delta\mathbf{n}(y)
to insert
\textup{ECT}(x)
,
\textup{ECT}(y)
, we obtain

\langle\varphi_{x}\varphi_{y}\rangle^{\mathfrak{p}}_{B_{n}}\langle\varphi_{x}\varphi_{y}\rangle^{0}_{B_{n}}\mathbf{P}^{xy,xy}_{B_{n}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\},B_{n}}[f(x,y,\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})\geq 2\text{ in }B_{m}]>c_{3}

for some uniform constant
c_{3}>0
.
Applying the switching principle of Theorem
3.12
we obtain

\langle\varphi_{x}^{2}\varphi_{y}^{2}\rangle^{\mathfrak{p}}_{B_{n}}\mathbf{P}^{xxyy,\emptyset}_{B_{n}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\},B_{n}}[f(x,y,\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})\geq 2\text{ in }B_{m}]>c_{3}.

We can now remove the sources
A=xxyy
at a finite cost by bounding
\Delta\mathbf{n}(x)
and
\Delta\mathbf{n}(y)
, and inserting
\textup{ECT}(x)
,
\textup{ECT}(y)
, to obtain

\mathbf{P}^{\emptyset,\emptyset}_{B_{n}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\},B_{n}}[f(x,y,\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})\geq 2\text{ in }B_{m}]>c_{4}

for some uniform constant
c_{4}>0
. Taking
n\to\infty
,

\mathbf{P}^{+,0}[f(x,y,\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathpzc{t})\geq 2]\geq c_{4}.

This contradicts (
6.3
) from Lemma
6.5
, thus concluding the proof of condition 4 in Proposition
2.15
and, hence, establishing (
1.9
) in Theorem
1.2
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
6.7
.

Assume for a contradiction that (
6.8
) does not hold. Then, there exist
x,y\in V
such that for every
m\geq 0
,

\mathbf{P}^{xy}_{B_{n}}[x\longleftrightarrow\partial B_{m}]\geq 1/2

for all
n
sufficiently large, since there is a path from
x
to
y
in
(\mathbf{n},\mathpzc{t})
due to the source constraints. We now wish to compare
\mathbf{P}^{\emptyset}_{B_{n}}[x\longleftrightarrow\partial B_{m}]
to
\mathbf{P}^{xy}_{B_{n}}[x\longleftrightarrow\partial B_{m}]
to deduce that the former remains bounded away from
0
, thus obtaining a contradiction. To this end, fix a sequence of vertices
u_{0}=x,u_{1},\ldots,u_{k}=y
such that
J_{u_{i},u_{i+1}}>0
for all
i=0,1,\ldots,k-1
, and let
M>0
be large enough (depending on
x,y
only) so that

\mathbf{P}^{xy}_{B_{n}}[x\longleftrightarrow\partial B_{m},\,\Delta\mathbf{n}(u_{i})\leq M,\>\forall i=0,1,,\ldots,k]\geq 1/4

(6.10)

for every
m\geq 0
and every
n
sufficiently large.
Now given a current
\mathbf{n}
with sources
x,y
, let
\mathbf{n}^{\prime}
be the sourceless current which is defined as

\mathbf{n}^{\prime}(s,t)=\begin{cases}\mathbf{n}(s,t)+1,&\text{for }\{s,t\}\in\big\{\{u_{0}u_{1}\},\ldots,\{u_{k-1}u_{k}\}\big\},\\
\mathbf{n}(s,t),&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

By (
3.2
), Proposition
4.1
and the fact that
\Delta\mathbf{n}(u_{i})\leq M
for all
i=0,1,\ldots,k
, we obtain

w_{\beta}^{xy}(\mathbf{n})\leq C_{5}w_{\beta}^{\emptyset}(\mathbf{n}^{\prime})\quad\text{and}\quad\rho_{\mathbf{n}}^{xy}[x\longleftrightarrow\partial B_{m}]\leq C_{5}\rho_{\mathbf{n}^{\prime}}^{\emptyset}[x\overset{(\mathbf{n}^{\prime},\mathpzc{t})}{\longleftrightarrow}\partial B_{m}]

for a constant
C_{5}>0
depending on
x,y
and
M
only.
Since the map
\mathbf{n}\mapsto\mathbf{n}^{\prime}
is injective, it follows that

\langle\varphi_{x}\varphi_{y}\rangle^{0}_{B_{n}}\mathbf{P}^{xy}_{B_{n}}[x\longleftrightarrow\partial B_{m},\,\Delta\mathbf{n}(u_{i})\leq M\,\forall i=0,1,\ldots,k]\leq C_{5}^{2}\mathbf{P}^{\emptyset}_{B_{n}}[x\longleftrightarrow\partial B_{m}].

By the irreducibility property, one can easily prove that
\langle\varphi_{x}\varphi_{y}\rangle^{0}_{B_{n}}\geq c_{6}
for
n
large enough, where
c_{6}>0
depends on
x,y
only. Recalling (
6.10
), we conclude that
\mathbf{P}^{\emptyset}_{B_{n}}[x\longleftrightarrow\partial B_{m}]\geq(c_{6}/4)C_{5}^{2}
for every
m
and every
n
large enough.
Taking
n\to\infty
and then
m\to\infty
, we obtain
\mathbf{P}^{0}[x\longleftrightarrow\infty]>0
, which concludes the proof by contradiction.
∎

Proof of Lemma
6.8
.

Let
x,x^{\prime}
be such that
c_{7}:=\mathbf{P}^{+}[\mathcal{A}_{x,x^{\prime}}]>0
. Then, by mixing we have

\lim_{y\to\infty}\mathbf{P}^{+}[\mathcal{A}_{x,x^{\prime}},\mathcal{A}_{y,y^{\prime}}]=c_{7}^{2},

where
y^{\prime}=\gamma\cdot x^{\prime}
for an automorphism
\gamma\in\Gamma
such that
\gamma\cdot x=y
. Thus, for every
y
sufficiently far away from
x
,
\mathbf{P}^{+}[\mathcal{A}_{x,x^{\prime}},\mathcal{A}_{y,y^{\prime}}]>c_{7}^{2}/2
. We now wish to modify our current so that it has sources
x,x^{\prime},y,y^{\prime}
. Assume without loss of generality that they are all distinct. Given
(\mathbf{n},\mathpzc{t})\in\mathcal{A}_{x,x^{\prime}}\cap\mathcal{A}_{y,y^{\prime}}
, let
(\mathbf{n}^{\prime},\mathpzc{t}^{\prime})
be the tangled current with sources
x,x^{\prime},y,y^{\prime}
defined as

\mathbf{n}^{\prime}(e)=\begin{cases}\mathbf{n}(e)-1,&\text{ for }e\in\{\{x,x^{\prime}\},\{y,y^{\prime}\}\},\\
\mathbf{n}(e),&\text{ otherwise },\end{cases}

where
\mathpzc{t}^{\prime}
coincides with
\mathpzc{t}
up to redefining
\overline{x},\overline{x}^{\prime},\overline{y},\overline{y}^{\prime}
to be the extra vertices in the blocks added due to the sources, similarly to the proof of Proposition
6.3
. Note that up to possibly exchanging
x
and
x^{\prime}
, and
y
and
y^{\prime}
,
(\mathbf{n}^{\prime},\mathpzc{t}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{B}_{m}(x,x^{\prime},y,y^{\prime})
for some
m>0
, where

\mathcal{B}_{m}(x,x^{\prime},y,y^{\prime}):=\{(\mathbf{n},\mathpzc{t}):\overline{x}\longleftrightarrow\overline{y}\text{ in }B_{m},\>\overline{x}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.75pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.75pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.625pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.875pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}\overline{x}^{\prime},\>\overline{y}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.75pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.75pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.625pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.875pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\longleftrightarrow}}}\overline{y}^{\prime}\},

and now
\overline{x},\overline{x}^{\prime},\overline{y},\overline{y}^{\prime}
denote the extra vertices in the blocks.
Thus a union bound implies that

\mathbf{P}^{+}[(\mathbf{n}^{\prime},\mathpzc{t}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{B}_{m}(x,x^{\prime},y,y^{\prime})]>c_{7}^{2}/8,

which in turn implies that

\mathbf{P}^{\emptyset}_{B_{n}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\}}[(\mathbf{n}^{\prime},\mathpzc{t}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{B}_{m}(x,x^{\prime},y,y^{\prime})]>c_{7}^{2}/8

for all
n
sufficiently large. After bounding the degrees, it follows that there exists a constant
c_{8}>0
independent of
x,x^{\prime},y,y^{\prime}
such that for every
n
large enough

\langle\varphi_{x}\varphi_{x^{\prime}}\varphi_{y}\varphi_{y^{\prime}}\rangle^{\mathfrak{p}}_{B_{n}}\mathbf{P}^{xx^{\prime}yy^{\prime}}_{B_{n}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\}}[\mathcal{B}_{m}(x,x^{\prime},y,y^{\prime})]\geq c_{8}\mathbf{P}^{\emptyset}_{B_{n}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\}}[(\mathbf{n}^{\prime},\mathpzc{t}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{B}_{m}(x,x^{\prime},y,y^{\prime})],

(6.11)

hence for every
n
sufficiently large,

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\partial\mathbf{n}=\{x,x^{\prime},y,y^{\prime}\}\end{subarray}}w_{\beta}^{xx^{\prime}yy^{\prime}}(\mathbf{n})\rho_{\mathbf{n}}^{xx^{\prime}yy^{\prime}}

\displaystyle[\mathcal{B}_{m}(x,x^{\prime},y,y^{\prime})]

\displaystyle\geq c_{8}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\partial\mathbf{n}={\emptyset}\end{subarray}}w_{\beta}^{\emptyset}(\mathbf{n})\rho_{\mathbf{n}}^{\emptyset}[(\mathbf{n}^{\prime},\mathpzc{t}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{B}_{m}(x,x^{\prime},y,y^{\prime})].

Our goal now is to prove that

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\partial\mathbf{n}=\{x,x^{\prime},y,y^{\prime}\}\end{subarray}}w_{\beta}^{xx^{\prime}yy^{\prime}}(\mathbf{n})\rho_{\mathbf{n}}^{xx^{\prime}yy^{\prime}}[\mathcal{B}_{m}(x,x^{\prime},y,y^{\prime})]\leq\\
\langle\varphi_{x^{\prime}}\varphi_{y^{\prime}}\rangle^{\mathfrak{p}}_{B_{n}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\partial\mathbf{n}=\{x,y\}\end{subarray}}w_{\beta}^{xy}(\mathbf{n})\rho_{\mathbf{n}}^{xx^{\prime}yy^{\prime}}[\mathcal{B}_{m}(x,x^{\prime},y,y^{\prime})].

(6.12)

Indeed, with this inequality and (
6.11
),

\displaystyle\frac{c_{7}^{2}}{8}c_{8}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\langle\varphi_{x}\varphi_{x^{\prime}}\varphi_{y}\varphi_{y^{\prime}}\rangle_{B_{n}}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{P}^{xx^{\prime}yy^{\prime}}_{B_{N}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\}}[\mathcal{B}_{m}(x,x^{\prime},y,y^{\prime})]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\langle\varphi_{x^{\prime}}\varphi_{y^{\prime}}\rangle_{B_{n}}^{\mathfrak{p}}\langle\varphi_{x}\varphi_{y}\rangle_{B_{n}}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{P}^{xy}_{B_{N}\sqcup\{\mathfrak{g}\}}[x\longleftrightarrow y\text{ in }B_{m}]

\displaystyle\leq
